| D1.18 | **The order-rejection ack carries an IBKR numeric error code across the seam** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 017 | unassigned. `_on_ib_error` calls `self._ack_once(cid, REJECTED, reason=f"{errorCode}: {errorString}")`, so a vendor integer reaches the seam inside `on_ack(reason)`. Invariant 2 says no IBKR code crosses the seam; ARC 017 enforced that on the **session** event (1100/1101/1102 removed from every session `reason` and moved to adapter-internal logging) but deliberately did **not** extend it to acks, because the parent scoped the change to the session path and because `on_ack(reason)` is the declared human-readable provenance channel where error 201's margin figure has real diagnostic value. So this is a genuine tension between invariant 2 and the provenance channel, not an oversight. Discharge alongside the Limiter, which is the first component that will actually consume an ack reason and can settle whether a structured rejection cause is owed the way `UP_DATA_LOSS` was owed for sessions. **Reported rather than substituting a decision.** **DISCHARGED ARC 018.** The deferral rationale in this row was **factually wrong and that error was the whole basis for deferring**: the Limiter is not "the first component that will actually consume an ack reason" — three consumers already read the ack `reason` slot by substring match (`test_broker_order.py:626`, `:1773`, `:1813`), and two of them match it to derive a *fact* (whether an ack was synthesised by §2c). So the field is neither structurally protected from programmatic consumption nor merely unconsumed; it is consumed today. Repair follows the `UP_DATA_LOSS` precedent: `RejectCategory` {`UNKNOWN`, `INSUFFICIENT_MARGIN`, `NOT_TRADABLE`, `VENUE_UNAVAILABLE`} is carried as a **keyword-only** `reject_category` on `on_ack`, so §2A's positional shape is untouched and the addition is visibly an addition; the frozen spec is not edited. `reason` keeps the full human text **including** the IBKR code, so error 201's margin figure — this row's own reason for the tension — is preserved. Invariant 2 is asserted mechanically two independent ways per rejection (the driving code appears in no structured field; and no structured field contains any digit at all), across an AST-derived list of every REJECTED-ack emission site rather than a hand-written one. Categories are justified by *distinct Limiter behaviour*, not by re-spelling IBKR's error list, and anything unmapped falls to `UNKNOWN` — including a 201 whose text does not carry the measured money wording, because IBKR's 201 is a wrapper and keying on the bare integer would make every 201 read as a money problem | discharged ARC 018 | broker-order |
| D1.19 | **Ack provenance — venue-vs-synthesised — is carried only as free text** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 018 | unassigned. Split out of D1.18 rather than folded into it, because it is a *different* defect that happens to share a field. `broker_order_ibkr.py` deliberately emits a genuine venue acceptance with **no** reason string, and marks a §2c-synthesised ack with the word `synthesised` in `reason`; the adapter's own comment endorses this as the mechanism. Three test sites read it back by substring (`test_broker_order.py:626`, `:1773`, `:1813`). That is `debug.md` §7.4's stale literal anchor — a *fact* a consumer needs, recoverable only by matching English in a free-text field. It is **not** an invariant-2 violation: `synthesised` is a Nix word, not a vendor spelling, which is exactly why ARC 018 fixed the vendor half (D1.18) and named this half instead of quietly widening its own scope. Discharge with the same shape that discharged D1.18 — a structured `AckProvenance {VENUE, SYNTHESISED}` beside `reject_category` — at the point a consumer needs it | unassigned — discharges with the Limiter or with any arc touching the §2c synthesis path | broker-order |
| D1.21 | **Seven `§`-citations outside the gated scope resolve to sections that do not exist** | ARC 008 numbering, renamed ARC 010, found ARC 019 | unassigned — discharges by widening `GOVERNED_ROOTS` in `checks/check_spec_citations.py`, which is a one-tuple edit, once somebody who may write those files corrects them. Found by the C1 gate on its first run and reported rather than reddened. All six cite the check contract by filename and then name a section it does not have — sections 9.1 and 9.2 at `scripts/verify.py:6` and `:7`, section 9.1 at `scripts/nixverify/__init__.py:1`, sections 9.3 and 9.4 at `scripts/tests/test_loader.py:1`, section 9.5 at `scripts/tests/test_systemd_units.py:1`, and section 9.1 at `install.sh:126`. **That document has no 9.1 through 9.5**: its section 9 is "Engine invariants" and its subsections are unnumbered. The numbers are carried over from the ARC 008 self-authored reconstruction that ARC 010 renamed *into* `nix_check_contract.md`, so the rename moved the filename and left the section numbering behind — the identical shape to the 2.1 phantom, one document over. The seventh is inside the check contract's own section 15 conformance map, which cites its own "2.1" and "2.4" as the anchors for doctrine C.1 and C.4; neither exists in it, and that file is a derived spec this arc does not own either. `install.sh:286-299` additionally attributes several of these to `elements_v2.md` by proximity, which is an attribution artefact rather than a separate defect and is noted here so the next reader does not count it twice. **Note the shape of the citations in this very row**: they deliberately avoid the section sigil next to a filename, because writing it would make this row itself a violation — which is the gate applying pressure in the correct direction, and is the mechanism D2.17 describes. **Not reddened in ARC 019** because sub-agent C's write scope is `checks/**` and `docs/CHECK-DEBT.md`, two sibling agents held `scripts/` at the time, and doctrine B.3 says an owner that cannot pay is no owner wearing a name | docs |
| D1.20 | **`_mirror_stale` latches across a successful reconnect** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 018 | unassigned. The flag is observable and correct at all three write sites and is read by three assertions, but `connect()` (`broker_order_ibkr.py`) discards `_rebuild_mirror()`'s verdict and never clears it, so a re-read that failed once stays `True` across a full reconnect that *did* rebuild successfully. It fails toward "suspect", which is the safe direction, so this is not urgent — but a consumer gating new entries on it would never resume trading after one failed re-read, making it a one-way door. It has no consumer today only because the Limiter does not exist. **Whoever builds that consumer must fix the latch in the same motion**; the required consumer semantics were written down in ARC 018 while the reasoning was fresh (treat `True` as HALT-class for new entries; do **not** let it suppress a protective flatten; pair it with `UP_DATA_LOSS` as level-to-edge). **ADAPTER HALF CLOSED ARC 019 (sub-agent A) — NARROWED, NOT DISCHARGED.** `connect()` now derives both `_mirror_stale` and the published session state from `_rebuild_mirror()`'s verdict, so a successful rebuild clears the flag and a failed one sets it; non-vacuity is proven by asserting the flag genuinely `True` immediately before the clearing reconnect, so "cleared" is a `True → False` transition and not a vacuous `False → False`. The same missing assignment was hiding a louder defect, fixed in the same motion: a **failed** cold-start rebuild used to publish plain `SessionState.UP` — the adapter reporting a healthy session over a mirror it did not rebuild, on the path §4 designates as ground truth. It now publishes `UP_DATA_LOSS`. A second laundering path was closed with it: a 1102 ("restored, data intact") arriving while `_mirror_stale` was already `True` used to wash a suspect mirror clean, and the level now wins over the edge. All emission sites are held to one mechanically-asserted invariant: plain `SessionState.UP` is published ONLY while `_mirror_stale` is `False`. **THE CONSUMER HALF REMAINS OPEN and is why this row does not close** | unassigned — consumer half discharges with the Limiter (R2) | broker-order |
| D1.22 | **A send verb cannot tell the caller whether the order reached the venue** | ARC 019 A1, measured | unassigned — discharges with the Limiter (R2). **NARROWED ARC 020 (A7), NOT DISCHARGED.** The adapter previously gave a consumer no way to know it was absorbing, so absorption had to be inferred from silence; `send_backlog()` now reports `vendor_queue_depth` (`ib_async.Client._msgQ`) and `transport_write_buffer_bytes` (the asyncio transport) as observable adapter state, with a `measured: bool` CANNOT-MEASURE floor so an unreadable pipe returns `None` and never `0` — a healthy idle pipe and a blind one stay distinguishable (`debug.md` failure mode #10). Every read is defensive and the method never raises, asserted against a `__len__` that does. **Deliberately NOT added to `ORDER_PORT_VERBS`**: a port verb binds every vendor, and whether a second venue can report its own queue depth is unmeasured — declaring a cross-vendor obligation from a sample of one is how a seam acquires a requirement nobody checked. Promotion is a `BrokerCapabilities` question for vendor two. **What stays open is the whole of the original row**: no bounded-queue policy was implemented (where the bound sits and what happens at it is the Limiter's), the resolution is still §4's pending-timeout state machine plus `query_order_status`, and **the repair is still never a resend**. Observability is not resolution. The send path provably does not block (16/16 verb×socket-condition cells returned under 0.0033 s against a real loopback socket with the real vendor serialiser), but the *reason* is that it **absorbs**: asyncio's transport buffers what the kernel will not take and `ib_async.Client.sendMsg` holds its own `_msgQ` in front of that. Measured directly — 200 `place_order` calls into a verified-saturated pipe returned in 0.032 s total, leaving 155 messages queued, 10204 B buffered and **zero bytes delivered to the peer**. Every one of those calls returned normally and none is known to have reached the venue. This is **not** a defect in the adapter and the repair is **never** a resend (§2A:71, §4:241, §12A:830). §4 already specifies the resolution — the pending-timeout state machine plus `query_order_status` — and both belong to the Limiter. The obligation is therefore a **consumer** one: treat a returned `place_order` as *submitted*, never as *accepted by the venue*; run a pending-ack timeout against it (`PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS`, §12A:830); resolve expiry by **querying** status. A bounded-queue-depth decision belongs at that point too, since today's queues are unbounded and a long stall trades a block for unbounded memory and an unbounded backlog of stale orders | broker-order |
| D1.23 | **A cancelled `connect()` leaves an adapter that accepts orders and can never report on them** | ARC 017 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B, T3-01) | **discharged ARC 020 (A2).** `connect()` now wraps session establishment in `except BaseException` and **re-raises**: cancellation is caught deliberately, because `CancelledError` is a `BaseException` and every `except Exception` in the file missed it, and it is re-raised untouched, because swallowing it would let `asyncio.wait_for` return normally from a call that did not complete — a different and worse defect than the one being fixed. A cancelled or failed `connect()` now leaves the adapter **refusing orders** (`_connected = False`, so `_require_session` raises `BrokerNotConnected`), with `_mirror_stale = True` because the rebuild verdict was never computed, and per-order state cleared. The refusal is observable on two channels, both asserted: `place_order` raises and nothing reaches the venue, and a cancelled *reconnect* over a previously-UP session publishes a genuine `DOWN` edge. A cancelled *first* connect publishes nothing, which is now correct rather than a gap — §2A defines `on_session` as connectivity **transitions**, and the caller's notification is the `CancelledError` it is about to receive. Non-vacuity asserts the cancel landed inside the intended window (`_connected is True` so not before `connectAsync`, `_startup_complete is False` so not after publish, and the rebuild's venue call proven entered). Can-fail four outputs; the `strict=True` xfail was removed in the same motion as the fix. Original finding, retained: `connect()` sets `_connected = True`, *then* awaits `_rebuild_mirror()`. `CancelledError` is a `BaseException`, so `_rebuild_mirror`'s `except Exception` does not catch it and nothing unwinds `_connected`. Any caller bounding the call with `asyncio.wait_for` — the ordinary way to bound a verb that talks to a venue — lands in that window. Blast radius measured: `place_order` **succeeds and reaches the venue** while `sink.acks == []`, `sink.fills == []`, `_mirror == {}`, and no `on_session` was ever published, so the consumer has been told nothing. The order path is live and mute. Held open by a `strict=True` xfail in `scripts/tests/test_broker_tier3.py`, so a repair turns the suite RED until the marker is removed in the same motion. **Not** addressed by D1.20's adapter fix — the rebuild verdict is never computed when the await is cancelled. Needs a decision about what a partially-completed `connect()` leaves behind, not a one-line fix | broker-order |
| D1.24 | **Per-order state is never released, and is not cleared across a session boundary** | ARC 014/017 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B; T3-01b, T3-02, T3-07, T3-08, T3-13) | **discharged ARC 020 (A1)**, and it was landed FIRST because D1.27(b)'s ownership gate is only sound on top of it. **Clearing happens at BOTH ends, and neither is redundant.** Teardown (`disconnect`, `_on_ib_disconnected`, `_abandon_session`) is where state is *released*, because teardown is the boundary a 03:00 Gateway restart actually arrives on and nothing guarantees a prompt reconnect — waiting for `connect()` would leave a live vendor `Order` object addressable for an unbounded interval. `connect()` clears again *before* `connectAsync`, because a process that connects without ever having disconnected has no teardown to have cleared it, and because that clear is what makes the ownership gate sound. **Retention rule:** terminal orders (`filled`/`cancelled`/`rejected`) are released after `terminal_order_retention_ms` = 20000 (10 × `PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS`), boot-validated to exceed both `PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` and `FILL_TIMEOUT_MS`. It cannot be zero for two independent §4 reasons: §4 "Failure resolution" resolves a pending timeout by *querying*, so the order must outlive the interval before the Limiter asks; and §4 "Partial fill" anticipates an execution arriving *after* a terminal transition. Pruning is lazy and amortised O(1) — no timer, no task. `_seen_execs` is released *with* the id map rather than separately: once the vendor mapping is gone a replayed execution is refused one step earlier, so the dedupe is subsumed, not lost. **THE IN-FLIGHT-AT-DROP CASE, which is the one that owed a real answer:** an order non-terminal when the session ended is neither deleted nor kept. Its per-order state is released and a `_Tombstone` retained holding `{client_order_id, last_known_state, cumulative_qty, session_seq}`. `query_order_status` returns `state="indeterminate"`, `terminal=False`, and the observed `cumulative_qty` floor — **§4:241's own third outcome, which this adapter previously could not reach at all.** It is not a lie in either direction: not `working` (a claim about a venue we can no longer see), not `unknown` (which would invite the Limiter to re-mint the id of an order that may be live). The id is neither re-mintable nor cancellable while the answer is outstanding; both raise `BrokerSeamError` naming the ended session and pointing at `query_order_status`. Terminal ids *are* released, so the refusal covers only ids that genuinely cannot be reused. `OrderStatus.state` gains `indeterminate` as a **declared Nix addition** on the `feed_lag()`/`UP_DATA_LOSS` precedent. Non-vacuity asserts the maps were genuinely populated before the boundary (non-empty → empty, not empty → empty) and that the fake genuinely recycled the vendor id, so the refusal costs something. Two `strict=True` xfails removed in the same motion. Original finding, retained — one shared repair, five observed symptoms. Measured: after **200 fully-closed lifecycles with the mirror netting flat**, `_neutral 200 · _orders 200 · _trades 200 · _to_ib 200 · _from_ib 200 · _acked 200 · _seen_execs 200` — nothing is pruned on a terminal transition (§12 non-stop operation; §13 objective 22 is a multi-day soak asserting memory stability). `connect()` clears `_to_ib`/`_from_ib`/`_acked`/`_cancelled` because IBKR ids reset across the Gateway's restart, but **not** `_orders`/`_trades`/`_neutral`/`_seen_execs`, and the consequences are concrete: (a) **`cancel_order` on a pre-restart id puts a live foreign order's `orderId` on the wire** — no race needed, just place / restart / place / cancel-the-first; (b) `query_order_status` returns the dead session's cached `working` forever, and §4:241 names exactly three outcomes (confirmed / cancelled / indeterminate) of which this adapter can reach two, because the only branch that can express uncertainty is reachable only when the trade is **absent**; (c) a Limiter minting deterministic neutral ids is blocked after a restart on an id that means nothing at the venue; (d) a futile cancel of a terminal order reaches the wire silently. Discharge needs a **retention policy** — how long must a terminal order stay queryable through `query_order_status`? — which is a §4 question, not a free deletion. Two of these are held open by `strict=True` xfails | broker-order |
| D1.25 | **Session state is published from a deferred task that never re-checks the session** | ARC 017 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B, T3-02b) | **discharged ARC 020 (A3)** by **two** independent mechanisms, each with its own can-fail, because one proved insufficient. (1) A single emission choke point, `_publish_session`, through which every `on_session` path now routes, enforcing two fail-closed rules: no UP-class state while `_connected` is False, and no non-transition `DOWN`. (2) A session epoch: `_revalidate_then_publish` carries the session it was scheduled in, and a rebuild completing into a *different* session publishes nothing and does not write `_mirror_stale` — a dead session's verdict must not land on a live session's flag. The verdict now gates **whether** to publish, not merely the `reason` string. The invariant is asserted **once, across all emission sites**: the test derives the emission-site list from the AST, asserts it has exactly one member named `_publish_session`, then wraps the sink and drives all seven publishing paths, asserting no UP-class state was ever published while `_connected` was False. AST scope is derived from `inspect.getsourcefile(IBKRBrokerOrder)` — the subject names its own file — to avoid failure mode #14. **A finding about this arc's own instrument, recorded because §7.12 asks it of every gate at the point it is built:** the first can-fail plant (removing the epoch check) **did not perturb** — the suite stayed green, because the only test driving that path drove the *disconnected* case, which the choke point catches on its own. Two mechanisms, one observable, and the weaker sufficed for everything being driven; that is failure mode #1 about a gate built in this arc. A traversal was added for the one sequence the choke point structurally cannot see — reconcile scheduled in session N, N torn down, N+1 established and UP — and the plant then perturbed. `strict=True` xfail removed in the same motion. Original finding, retained: `_on_data_loss_restore` schedules `_revalidate_then_publish` and returns; a `disconnect()` landing in that gap is enough to publish `UP_DATA_LOSS` (`is_up=True`) over a torn-down session, observed with `_connected=False`. The rebuild verdict selects only the `reason` **string** — it never gates the publish at all. `SessionState.is_up` is documented as the canonical spelling of "is the session usable", so this reads True over a session that is not, and it fails toward **resuming**, the direction §14 forbids. Adjacent to but **not** fixed by D1.20's ARC 019 work, which closed the cold-start and 1102 laundering paths and did not touch the deferred-task path. Held open by a `strict=True` xfail | broker-order |
| D1.26 | **Two overlapping `query_positions()` calls lose the newer snapshot** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B, T3-10) | **discharged ARC 020 (A5).** Guard chosen: **a monotonic sequence taken per read *before* the await; a read resolving with a number lower than the newest already-resolved read is discarded.** Serialising reads behind a lock was considered and **rejected**, and the reason is the protective path: a lock makes one read *wait* for another, and `_rebuild_mirror`'s completion is what clears `_mirror_stale`, which is what `flatten` sizes against — so a lock would put an unrelated caller's round trip in front of the protective path's input, which §2A's "`flatten` must not block" forbids. **No await was added to the protective path, and none could have been**: the guard is one `next()` on an `itertools.count` and one integer comparison, both on the already-async read path, and `flatten()` does not call `query_positions` at all — it reads `_mirror` from memory, as it always has. The sequence advances on *resolution*, including when the pre-existing fills-guard declines to apply, because a skip is itself a newer decision about the field. The **returned list is deliberately unguarded**: a caller gets the snapshot it asked for, and the guard protects the shared field two readers can corrupt for each other. Non-vacuity asserts from the fake's own record that both reads genuinely entered, that neither had completed at setup, and that the completion order really was reversed — the trap ARC 019's traversal hit twice, where a sequence never actually interleaved. `strict=True` xfail removed in the same motion. Original finding, retained: `self._mirror` is assigned wholesale with no ordering guard, so a read issued first but completing last overwrites a newer venue-confirmed state; observed `mirror={}` after the venue had already confirmed `+3`. The module produces the concurrent second read **itself** — `_on_data_loss_restore` schedules one and §4 makes a cold-start one mandatory. The same method already guards the *adjacent* case, snapshotting `_seen_execs` across the await with a comment saying a fill landing between request and assignment would be silently erased: fills are guarded, a concurrent read of the same field is not. This is the protective path's only input. Held open by a `strict=True` xfail | broker-order |
| D1.27 | **Two `flatten()` sequences have no specified correct behaviour — SPEC GAPS, not code defects** | found ARC 019 (sub-agent B, T3-04 and T3-05) | **RE-DISPOSED ARC 020 — STILL OPEN, NOT DISCHARGED.** This cell read *"architect, not ARC 020"* until ARC 020 began, and that was **stale rather than wrong**: it was written in ARC 019 before the operator had ruled, and ARC 020's brief carries **both** ratified operator rulings — one for the idempotence gap (a) and one for the protective-flatten-during-reconnect gap (b) — which are exactly the two things this row said only the architect could supply. The rulings were therefore available to this arc, and this row pointed the reader away from them. **Disposition now:** sub-agent A implements both in ARC 020 as **declared Nix additions** following the `feed_lag()` / `UP_DATA_LOSS` precedent, and each ruling is recorded verbatim, attributed as an operator ruling in ARC 020 rather than as spec text, in the pending-amendment record. **The row stays open, and the reason is the row's own subject.** What it records is a gap in a **frozen document**, and behaviour landing in Nix code does not make that document say anything. Only a v1.4 closes it, that is an architect action, and until it lands the specified behaviour and the implemented behaviour are two different things — which is the condition this row exists to keep visible. Discharge is a v1.4 carrying both rulings; the owner of the discharge is the architect, and the owner of the *implementation* was this arc. ARC 019 deliberately did not invent the answers, and did not have to. **BOTH HALVES ARE NOW IMPLEMENTED, AND THIS ROW IS STILL OPEN — the disagreement was real and is recorded rather than smoothed.** ARC 020's sub-agent A reported (a) and (b) as *discharged*; sub-agent C, which owns this ledger, re-disposed the row as *still open*. **Phase 4 adjudicated in C's favour, and the reason is the row's own subject.** What this row records is a gap in a **frozen document**. Behaviour landing in Nix code does not make that document say anything, and closing the row on an implementation would leave the specified behaviour and the implemented behaviour silently divergent — which is the exact condition this row exists to keep visible. Prohibition 1 and the pending-amendment procedure both place the v1.4 with the architect. (a) landed as the bounded flatten idempotency window, `flatten_idempotency_window_ms` = 2000, **derived as = `PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS`** (§12A:830, "~1–2s", upper end taken) and living in `risks/broker_order.config.json` rather than as a literal on the protective path: the window suppresses a repeat protective flatten for exactly as long as the Limiter is still waiting for the first flatten's ack, and at that instant the §4 pending-timeout machinery takes the question over, so suppressing past it would let the adapter refuse to protect while nothing else was protecting either. Four cross-knob boot-validation rules, all can-fail demonstrated; a missing or invalid config raises rather than defaulting. (b) landed as the ownership discriminator in `_startup_admits`. Both rulings are recorded **verbatim** in the code and in `docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md`, attributed as operator rulings issued in ARC 020 rather than as spec text. (a) **`flatten()` is not idempotent:** two protective flattens over one `+2` mirror emit **−4** of market orders, because `place_order` does not decrement the mirror and only a fill does. §2A's `flatten(symbol \| all)` bullet defines the verb as "market-close a position (protective path; must not block)" and is **silent on repeat invocation**; §4 lists six *independent* protective triggers, unconditional, so two firing in one cycle is a designed shape rather than a caller error, and §4's "one in-flight action per strategy" governs strategy signals, not Limiter-side protective exits. The section that would have to say is §2A's `flatten` bullet or §4 "Exits (dual authority)" — **neither currently does.** (b) **A protective `flatten()` during reconnect fires into a shut startup gate:** the fan-out proceeds (`_connected` is set before the rebuild) and every ack and fill for those orders is refused by the still-closed gate. §14's "the exit/protective path has zero wire/delivery dependency" is honoured literally — nothing blocked — and not in the sense that matters, because the outcome is unobservable. The section that would have to say is §4 "Boot / known-state discipline", which gates *registration* on the cold-start query and is silent on a protective exit during session **re-establishment**, which is exactly when one is most likely | broker-order |
| D1.28 | **The protective path cannot report that it failed to achieve its purpose** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B; T3-06, T3-11, T3-12) | **NARROWED ARC 020 (A6), NOT DISCHARGED — the adapter half is done and the consumer half is untouched.** All three surprises are now observable. `flatten` produces a `FlattenAttempt` record following `place_order`'s local-receipt pattern — the §2A `-> None` signature is untouched, the record is retained internally and read back through `last_flatten_attempt()` / `flatten_attempts()` (bounded ring, depth 64) — carrying per-attempt `intents`, `suppressed` (with prior cid, prior attempt seq, age and window), `no_position`, `failures`, and `mirror_stale` captured **at attempt time**. `is_silent_no_op` is the readable spelling of (b). An unheld symbol logs loudly and records but deliberately **does not raise**: §4's flatten-on-uncertainty "may hit nothing OR close a real position", so raising would make the safe case look like a fault, and failure mode #11 asks for an observable, not a throw. For (c), `disconnect()`'s teardown stays unconditional and best-effort while only the *emission* becomes edge-correct, enforced at `_publish_session` so no future site can reopen it; UP-class states are deliberately not collapsed, because a repeated `UP_DATA_LOSS` carries a fresh reconciliation obligation. **WHY THIS IS NOT DISCHARGED: nothing reads `FlattenAttempt`.** The record is the adapter's declaration of **intent**, never a claim about the venue — D1.22 proves intent and arrival are different facts — and intent-versus-outcome reconciliation is the Limiter's (R2). A reconciler was explicitly not built. Consumer obligations recorded: `suppressed` means *a protective action was requested and deliberately not sent*, not that it was sent; `is_silent_no_op` together with `mirror_stale` is the flatten-on-uncertainty trigger; `intents` must be reconciled against venue truth. This consumer half now sits alongside D1.20's. Original finding, retained — consumer-adjacent. Three surprises that are individually defensible and together mean a caller cannot distinguish success from silence. (a) `flatten()` sizes from a mirror snapshot and never re-checks: a fill landing one microsecond after the fan-out leaves the protective order under-sized — observed mirror `+2` against a `SELL 1` — and **nothing reports that the protective action fell short**; §4's indeterminate path does have the Limiter reconcile afterward, but the adapter gives it no signal that a reconciliation is owed, so this belongs with D1.20's consumer obligation. (b) `flatten(symbol)` on an unheld symbol returns None, places nothing, raises nothing and logs nothing, so on the protective path "already flat" and "the mirror has lost this position" are indistinguishable — and the adapter holds `_mirror_stale`, which already knows which world it may be in; contrast the per-symbol failure path a few lines below in the same method, which collects and raises with the full picture (`debug.md` failure mode #11 / doctrine C.7). (c) `disconnect()` emits `on_session(DOWN)` on every call including when there was never a session, while `nics_risk_subsystem_spec_v1.3.md` §2A defines `on_session` as connectivity **transitions** — fails toward halted, so characterisation rather than alarm, and it pairs with the edge-versus-level distinction already recorded for `_mirror_stale` | broker-order |
| D1.29 | **`Balance.cash`, `Balance.maint_margin` and `Balance.init_margin` carry two different meanings depending on which writer wrote last** | ARC 014/017 code, found ARC 020 (A8, the per-writer field-meaning rule) | unassigned — adapter-and-seam decision with a consumer half, so it discharges with R2 or with a seam change R2 has agreed to. `query_balance` populates all four figures from `accountSummaryAsync`. `_on_ib_account_value` — the **push** path, which is what `on_balance` actually delivers — writes `0.0` for `cash`, `maint_margin` and `init_margin`, because it fires on the `NetLiquidation` tag and has nothing else to report. So a consumer reading `on_balance(...).cash` receives `0.0`, which is not a missing value but a **confident lie**, and it is a lie in precisely the shape of the two `avg_price` incidents: both writers are individually plausible, and only a new *class* of test could expose the disagreement. §14 makes this load-bearing rather than cosmetic — *"Survival is watched on net-liq; sizing is computed on cash. Never conflate."* A Limiter sizing on a pushed `cash` of `0.0` computes a size of zero, or divides by it. **Deliberately not repaired in ARC 020**, because both candidate fixes — `Optional[float]` per figure, or a `partial: bool` on `Balance` — are seam changes whose consumer half is R2's, and the standing rule for this arc was enumerate, assert, report, not resolve. **It is PINNED by assertion**: the current per-writer meanings are asserted per writer, so the day a writer starts populating those fields the suite reddens and the decision is forced rather than made silently. Discharge is a decision about whether the seam distinguishes "not reported" from "zero", not a one-line fix | broker-order |

ARC 020's A5 closed *read-versus-read* on `query_positions` with a monotonic sequence, and the pre-existing fills-guard covers *fill-versus-read*. **Push-versus-fill has no guard at all**: a `positionEvent` snapshot older than an already-applied fill would overwrite it, and `positionEvent` carries no venue sequence to order by — §2A invariant 4 is unsatisfiable on IBKR at this surface. ## D2 — Doctrine rules Nix does not yet satisfy

From `nix_check_contract.md` §15.3.

| # | doctrine | gap | owner | owning module |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D2.1 | A.3 | no `status` surface; when built it must be a view over `verify`, never a second program | unassigned | verify |
| D2.2 | A.5 | the directory tree is prose in `directory_structure.md`, not a declared checkable state — `verify --correct` cannot move a stray file home | unassigned | verify |
| D2.3 | A.6 | no release driver and no update driver, so `verify` gates no irreversible act | unassigned | verify |
| D2.4 | B.1 | nothing runs `verify.py` at an arc boundary and banks its verdict. Nix has no `bank.sh`; pre-commit covers per-commit only | unassigned | verify |
| D2.5 | B.3 | no `known-red` marker mechanism, so an unexpected RED cannot be discriminated from an expected one | unassigned | verify |
| D2.6 | B.6 | no `prove_*` harnesses (determinism, live/replay byte-identity, clock purity, crash-safety) | unassigned — blocked until a trading core exists | verify |
| D2.7 | B.6 | nothing is baselined on a pristine tree before an arc begins. **ARC 010 is the measured instance:** bandit had been scanning nothing since ARC 006 and no baseline existed to catch it | unassigned | verify |
| D2.8 | B.7 | **discharged ARC 017** — `checks/check_derived_claims.py` + `checks/derived_claims.json`. Seven claims, each a set of **commands that compute a number at run time**; the registry stores **no integer anywhere**, because banking "16" beside the claim that §2A has 16 elements would rebuild the defect the instrument exists to catch. Every claim needs ≥2 sources and the gate is CANNOT-MEASURE (exit 2) if a claim has one source or two sources that are the same computation. Can-fail demonstrated three ways, each naming claim/stated/derived: a wrong series figure, a spec-vs-code drift firing two claims at once in both B.7 directions, and a missing file (**FAIL, not skip**). Its first live act was to correct this ledger's own series row — see the note above. **Known limit, stated beside the gate rather than papered over:** it proves every *registered* number is right and cannot prove the registry covers the numbers that matter (failure mode #14, inherent to a registry-driven instrument). The ARC 016 note below remains correct — D2.8 was never the item promoted to §7.12 | — | verify |
| D2.9 | C.5 | the stdlib-only scan is textual, not proof-by-absence over the import closure | unassigned | verify |
| D2.10 | C.6 | no verdict-by-verdict comparison harness | unassigned — nothing to compare yet | verify |
| D2.11 | C.10 | no named owner for shared global measurements (tree-wide complexity, lint state) | unassigned | verify |
| D2.12 | D.6 | `test_runner_coverage.py` proves every registered check is *reachable*, not that a registered gate which should fail actually reddens a real run end to end. **A suite that silently skips a gate reports GREEN** | unassigned | verify |
| D2.13 | §7.12 / D.6 | **discharged ARC 018** — the `pytest-affected` entry is no longer bare `pytest --testmon`; it is a runtime-gate program that makes the state of `.testmondata` an input to the verdict and prints `SELECTED=` on every run. Zero selection is no longer terminal: it is named (`NOTHING-SELECTED`, `SCOPE-BLIND`, `SELECTOR-BROKEN`, all non-zero exits, exit 2 kept distinct from exit 1 per B.2) and then escalated to a non-incremental run so the commit is measured rather than waved through. Proven in order on this tree: **non-vacuity** (cold database → `collected 159 items`); **defect reproduced pre-fix** (warm, no change → `changed files: 0` / `collected 0 items` / `no tests ran` / hook **Passed** / exit 0); **fix under identical conditions** (hook **Failed**, exit code 2, `RUNTIME-GATE verdict: NOTHING-SELECTED`; with escalation on, `mode=full-escalated(zero-selection) SELECTED=159 MEASURED-PASS` in 11.6 s); **D3.4's selection proof re-run intact** (plant `assert 1 == 2` → `collected 9 items`, neither 0 nor the whole suite, `FAILED …test_arc018_plant_testmon` naming `scripts/tests/test_check_venv.py:214`, exit 1 → restored to sha256 `fd5d4992…877b5` → `collected 8 items`, pass). `__pycache__` purged between every step. **Two findings came out of building it, and both are worse than the row as written.** (i) A tracked source file can change and select **nothing**: `scripts/nixverify/__init__.py` is in scope for every other hook and has **no fingerprint at all** in testmon's graph, so appending a line to it produced `changed files: 0` / `collected 0 items` / exit 0 — the old gate was green over a real, staged, tracked change. (ii) testmon does not notice its own corrupted record: overwriting one `file_fp.fsha` with zeroes still produced `changed files: 0` / `collected 0 items` / exit 0. The new gate catches both, the second by recomputing the git-blob hashes testmon stores rather than by asking testmon what changed. **Residual, stated rather than discovered later:** `.testmondata` is still untracked and still per-machine — tracking it was rejected (binary SQLite plus WAL sidecars is a second source of truth that goes stale on write; ARC 016 rejected the same shape for `downloads/*.py`), so what changed is that its state is now visible and load-bearing, not that it became reviewable. See **D2.16** for the debt this repair itself opened. **PHASE 4 CORRECTION — the first design of this fix was wrong, and integration found it.** `SELECTOR-BROKEN` originally *terminated* on `drift and selected == 0`. Reproduction is one line: append a comment to any test file and run the gate twice. Both runs print `SELECTOR-BROKEN … exit 1`, identically — it does **not** self-clear, so a commit touching only comments or docstrings was permanently red with a verdict naming a selector that was working correctly. The cause is a real asymmetry rather than a bug in either tool: this gate's corroboration is **content-based** (a git blob hash), while testmon's selection is **semantic** (per-method checksums), so a behaviour-neutral edit legitimately changes the content hash while changing no method checksum. Content drift is evidence the record is *stale*; that is not the same claim as *the selector is broken*, and the original arm conflated them. Repaired by escalating instead of terminating: the full run measures everything, so a subsequent pass is honest, `drift=` still names every affected file on the scope line, and the arm stays terminal under `NIX_RUNTIME_GATE=noescalate` — which is how the A2-ii corrupted-`fsha` demonstration was taken in the first place, so that evidence is unaffected. Re-proved after the repair: comment-only edit → `mode=full-escalated(SELECTOR-BROKEN:…) SELECTED=180 → MEASURED-PASS exit 0`, and the next run shows `drift=0` — it self-clears. Can-fail re-proved on the final gate: plant → `SELECTED=9` (neither 0 nor 181) → `FAIL` naming the site → restore to sha256 `fd5d4992…` → `SELECTED=8` → `MEASURED-PASS`. **This is the argument for D2.16's discharge: the defect was invisible while the program lived in a YAML string and surfaced within minutes of it becoming testable code** | — | hooks |
| D2.14 | `nics_risk_subsystem_spec_v1.3.md` §2A:71 / §4:241 / §12A:830 · doctrine B.4 | **A hand-rolled retry loop on the order path is banned and undetected.** `checks/check_order_path_bans.py` (ARC 017) proves *no retry **library*** (`tenacity`/`backoff`/`retrying`) and *no loop-blocking **call***; it does **not** prove nothing retries. `for _ in range(3): self.place_order(...)` passes both arms. §2.1 exists because a retry on the order path turns one intended order into two, and that is a semantic property, not an import. A PASS from that gate must be read as exactly what it measures — the gate's own docstring says so under §7.12 condition 6, and this row is the ledger half of that admission. Discharge needs a semantic instrument (call-graph reachability from `place_order` back to itself, or an AST loop-containing-a-send analysis), which is a different and harder gate. **CITATION CORRECTED ARC 018 — this row cited §2.1 of the frozen spec and there is no §2.1.** Headings run `## 2.` → `## 2A.`; the only `x.1` headings in the file are 12.1/12.10/12.11. “§2.1” was the ARC 017 *brief's* prohibition 1 — a task document — propagated into this row and into `check_order_path_bans.py`'s own docstring. The ban is real; the anchor was not. Real anchors, all verified on disk: §2A:71 `query_order_status` — “pending-timeout resolution (**never auto-resend**)”; §4:241 “issues order-status query, **never auto-resends**”; §12A:830 `PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` — “status query, **never resend**”. **NARROWED ARC 018, NOT DISCHARGED.** `check_order_path_bans` arm (iii) now detects three retry shapes structurally by AST — `loop_contains_send`, `bounded_counter_send`, `except_reinvokes_send` — with can-fail proven per shape and a per-site `(file, qualname, shape, verb)` suppression registry that self-expires. Measured false-positive rate on the merged tree: **1 hit over 9 loops in 4 files**, and that one hit (`IBKRBrokerOrder.flatten`'s per-symbol fan-out) is a site a reviewer should see. **Three residuals keep this row open, each named beside the gate:** retry by *recursion* (`except: return self._send(n-1)` — fires no shape); indirection deeper than one hop (`INDIRECTION_DEPTH = 1`); and retry across a thread or process boundary, which no scanner can see. Discharge still needs the call-graph reachability the row originally named; arm (iii) is the cheap two-thirds of it | unassigned — the three residuals | verify |
| D2.16 | C.10 / §7.12 / #14 | **The runtime gate's own program is outside every gate.** Opened by ARC 018's own repair, stated rather than left to be found. The `pytest-affected` verdict logic — database audit, independent hash recomputation, verdict taxonomy, escalation — is a Python program living inside the `entry:` string of `.pre-commit-config.yaml`. `ruff`, `pylint`, `mypy`, `bandit` and `complexipy` all see a YAML file; none of them see that Python, and no pytest test drives it. So the instrument that decides whether the runtime was measured is the one artifact in the tree with no static check and no test over it, which is failure mode #14 aimed at the gate itself. It is there because ARC 018 scoped sub-agent A's writes to `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `pyproject.toml`, `scratch/**` and this ledger — `scripts/` was forbidden, and a gate that must exist somewhere ends up wherever it is allowed. Discharge: lift the program verbatim into a tracked `scripts/runtime_gate.py`, point `entry:` at it, and give it tests — at which point every hook covers it and the §7.12 answer 7 in the config header can be deleted rather than reworded. **DISCHARGED ARC 018 Phase 4, by the parent, which owns `scripts/`.** The program was lifted verbatim to `scripts/runtime_gate.py` and `entry:` now reads `./.venv/bin/python scripts/runtime_gate.py`. It is covered by all eight hooks (ruff-check, ruff-format, pylint, mypy, bandit, complexipy and the runtime gate itself) and by `scripts/tests/test_runtime_gate.py`, whose first assertion is a non-vacuity check deriving the invoked path **out of `.pre-commit-config.yaml`** so the suite cannot drift onto an orphan module. **The lift paid for itself immediately:** static analysis of the now-visible code produced bandit B405/B314/B607 and pylint C0209/R0913 findings that the YAML string had hidden, and integration testing of the now-testable code found the `SELECTOR-BROKEN` false positive recorded under D2.13. **COUNT CORRECTED ARC 023 (B2), MEASURED NOT READ: it is 7 of 8 hooks, not 8.** pre-commit's own `Classifier` over `git.get_all_files()` selects `scripts/runtime_gate.py` for `ruff-check`, `ruff-format`, `pylint`, `mypy`, `bandit (production)` and `complexipy`, plus `pytest-affected` which is `always_run` — **`bandit (tests)` does not select it**, because its `files: ^scripts/tests/` excludes everything outside the test tree. The sentence above names SEVEN hooks in its own parenthetical while claiming eight, so it contradicted itself where it stood. **This is D3.1's defect verbatim, eight arcs later** — a coverage claim recorded against two bandit instruments when only one of them can see the file — and it is why B.7's derive-never-restate applies to coverage claims and not only to numbers. The substantive control is unaffected: six static hooks plus the companion test genuinely read the module, and `scripts/tests/test_runtime_gate.py` genuinely derives its import target out of `.pre-commit-config.yaml`. The discharge stands; the number did not | discharged ARC 018 | hooks |
| D2.15 | C.10 / §7.4 | **`check_order_path_bans` scans one directory, and nothing guards that the order path still lives there.** Scope derives at run time from `ORDER_PATH_DIRS = ("scripts/broker",)` via `rglob`, so a *new file* under `scripts/broker/` is covered automatically — the moving-value trap the gate was written to avoid. But a new *home* is not: an adapter added at `scripts/risk/` or `scripts/limiter/` is silently outside the scan while both required members remain present and non-vacuity still passes, so the gate stays green having never looked. §7.12 condition 4 beside the gate, unguarded and named there. This is the same shape as D2.11 (no owner for shared global measurements) but concrete. Discharge with whichever arc first puts order-path code outside `scripts/broker/`, which is also the moment it becomes discoverable. **NARROWED ARC 018, NOT DISCHARGED.** Scope no longer comes from a path anyone typed: the gate derives it from **file content** — every directory holding a module that declares the order port (defines `ORDER_PORT_VERBS`, or a class carrying ≥ 4 roster-verb methods), read from each file's own AST, union'd with `ORDER_PATH_DIRS` as a floor so the scan can never collapse *below* its historical scope. The name `ORDER_PATH_DIRS` is deliberately kept so this row's own citation does not go stale in the act of discharging it. Proven: an adapter planted at `scripts/limiter/` moved the scan from `4 files over 1 dirs` to `5 files over 2 dirs` and was both found and judged; the file set is registered as claim `order_path_scope_files` so a silent change is visible. **What keeps it open: a module that CALLS the order port without DECLARING it.** The future Limiter is exactly that shape and is the likeliest home of a real hand-rolled retry. Closing it needs call-site derivation, which multiplies the false-positive surface by the size of the Limiter — that trade is named here, not made | unassigned — the call-site case, discharges with the Limiter | verify |

| D2.17 | C.10 / §7.4 / #14 | **An UNATTRIBUTED `§`-citation is the defect that produced §2.1, and `check_spec_citations` treats it as an advisory.** Opened ARC 019 by the gate that closed the attributed half, stated rather than left to be found. The gate resolves a citation only when the surrounding block names the document; a citation naming no document is counted, printed on every run, and never a violation. But "banned by §2.1", with no document, is *exactly* how a task brief's own prohibition acquired frozen-spec authority — the reader supplies the document. **Measured ARC 019 on the merged tree: 1144 citations scanned, 136 attributed, 958 unattributed.** So the class this gate was built for is, today, ~7× larger outside its judgement than inside it. It is an advisory rather than a violation because promoting it in one step reddens files across four owners simultaneously, which is a blocked branch rather than a finding. Discharge: promote unattributed to a violation **inside `GOVERNED_ROOTS` first**, after the citations there carry their document — the repair is to write the filename beside the citation, at which point the existing gate starts checking it with no edit to the gate. The trend is readable in the meantime because both counts print on every run | verify |
| D2.18 | B.4 / §7.4 | **Citation attribution is a proximity heuristic, so the gate proves a citation is not DANGLING and cannot prove it is APT.** Opened ARC 019 beside D2.17 because it is the other half of the same limit and folding them together would make one look covered by the other's discharge. `§13` is a real heading in four of the five indexed documents; which one a given `§13` means is decided by the nearest document alias within the enclosing structural block, bounded at 160 characters. | D2.20 | C.10 / §7.4 | **`check_datafeed_granted_mode` arm B3 is name-identity, not dataflow.** Opened ARC 021 Phase 4 by the repair that created the limit, stated rather than left to be found. Phase 4 excluded `self` and `cls` because every method call contributes the receiver to both sets, which made the arm redden a correct adapter. The residual is that a grant laundered through a rename is caught only when the rename shares a name: `m = self._requested_mode; ... granted_mode=m` is caught, an unrelated-looking attribute is not. **MEASURED, not theorised** — a plant substituting `requested_mode` for `granted_mode` inside the adapter-wide accessor passed this arm. Arm B1's behavioural drive is the compensating control, and the pytest pair added in Phase 4 is what actually fails that plant. **ASSESSED ARC 022 (C4) — CLOSURE REFUSED, WITH THE COST ARGUED RATHER THAN ESTIMATED, AND THE ROW STAYS OPEN.** The refusal follows ARC 020's D3.7 standard: a named gap is worth more than a weak closure. **What closing it would actually require.** B3's question is *does the value reported as granted derive from the value requested*. That is a dataflow question and the current answer is set intersection over identifiers. Making it dataflow needs reaching-definitions that are (a) **field-sensitive** — the real adapter parks the grant in per-symbol state, so `self._symbols[symbol].granted` must be distinguishable from `self._requested_mode`; (b) **inter-procedural** — the write happens in a vendor callback and the read happens in an accessor, two methods that never appear in one another's AST; and (c) **callback-entry aware** — the value enters as a parameter of a function the adapter never calls itself, so no call site connects the definition to the use. That is a static analyser, not a gate arm, and this project has already refused this exact trade twice on the same grounds: D2.15 refused call-site derivation because it multiplies the false-positive surface by the size of the code it would have to read, and D2.18 refused semantic matching of citation text because it means reading intent out of prose. Building it here would put a third such engine in a boot-time check that must stay inside its measured 0.4 s. **The cheap approximations were considered and each is a doctrine B.4 hazard rather than a saving.** Widening `_granted_names` beyond the `granted_mode=` keyword to any attribute whose name contains `grant` is a spelling rule wearing a correctness gate's exit code, and it reddens on the first adapter that calls the field something else. Requiring the granted value to come from a vendor callback parameter is a shape rule keyed to *this* vendor, which is the D3.10 defect being manufactured a second time. **A MEASUREMENT TAKEN WHILE ASSESSING, WHICH STRENGTHENS THE REFUSAL AND IS WHY IT IS RECORDED HERE:** run against today's tree, arm B3 reports `request-call names ['_requested_mode', 'value']` for the real adapter and its `granted` side is **empty**, because the real adapter has no `granted_mode=` keyword anywhere — the grant arrives as a callback parameter into per-symbol state. So on its real subject this arm is not merely approximate, it is **vacuous**, which is D3.10 restated in the arm's own output. The residual is therefore larger than this row said, and the honest disposition is to leave it open and say so rather than to close it on an approximation. **SWEEP RESULT ARC 023 (B2): THIS ROW'S FIRST COMPENSATING CONTROL DOES NOT MEASURE.** *"Arm B1's behavioural drive is the compensating control"* — arm B1 is D3.16's subject and it has never once driven the adapter's `granted_mode`. Re-measured on today's tree, all three legs return `AttributeError: 'int' object has no attribute '_symbols'` and the gate is CANNOT_MEASURE. The SECOND control this row names — the ARC 021 Phase 4 pytest pair — was checked and **does** measure: both cases exist, drive the real adapter, and carry their plant in their own docstrings. So the residual is compensated by a test and not by the gate, which is materially weaker than the row read, and D3.10 is the reason that distinction matters. **THE AIM QUESTION, ASKED OF THIS ROW IN ARC 023 (S2) AND ANSWERED YES — WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF D2.21's ANSWER AND IS WORTH RECORDING FOR THAT REASON.** D3.15's sweep established that a compensating control must be checked for AIM before EXISTENCE. This row's first control now EXISTS (arm B1 rebuilt, D3.16), so the aim question is live: *would a working arm B1 actually cover a grant laundered through an unrelated-looking attribute — the residual B3 cannot see?* **YES, and it is measured rather than argued.** Arm B1 is behavioural: it subscribes with no grant callback and asserts the reported mode is the sentinel, so ANY derivation of the reported value from the requested one — by rename, by attribute, by anything — reports a mode where the sentinel is owed and fails. ARC 021's plant 2 is exactly that laundering and it is now CAUGHT, naming `granted_mode()@subscribed-ungranted` and `granted_mode()@divergent-grants`; so is P3, which launders it one level deeper inside the grant callback. Arm B1 does not read names at all, which is precisely why it reaches what B3's name identity cannot. **THE ROW STAYS OPEN AND THE REFUSAL STANDS.** B3 is still name-identity, its granted-side set is still EMPTY on this adapter, and this arc did not build the field-sensitive inter-procedural dataflow the ARC 022 assessment costed. What changed is that the residual is now compensated by an instrument that measures AND is aimed at it, instead of by a sentence naming one that never ran | unassigned — needs field-sensitive inter-procedural dataflow, refused ARC 022, compensating control real since ARC 023 | verify |
| D2.21 | C.10 / §7.12 | **`check_datafeed_bar_seal` arm 2 does not check guard POLARITY.** Opened ARC 021 Phase 4 alongside the repair that taught the arm a second guard spelling. The arm now recognises both the membership form and the lookup-then-sentinel form, and treats a store as guarded if either dominates it — but it does not distinguish `if found is None` from `if found is not None`, exactly as it never distinguished `in` from `not in` (the gate's own §7.12 condition 5). An inverted guard would overwrite every sealed entry and only ever store on the re-poll. The residual is UNCHANGED IN SIZE by the repair rather than introduced by it, which is the reason it is recorded here rather than treated as new damage. Arm 4's behavioural drive is the compensating control. **DISCHARGED ARC 022 (C4), AND THE COMPENSATING CONTROL THIS ROW LEANED ON TURNED OUT NOT TO EXIST — SEE D3.15.** `_guard_keys` is replaced by `_absent_proofs`, which answers a test with TWO sets instead of one: what it proves ABSENT when true, and what it proves absent when false. The `If` handler applies the first to `body` and the second to `orelse`, so a seal is credited only on the branch where the key is known not to be there. **WHY IT CANNOT BE A WEAKENING, STRUCTURALLY AND NOT BY ASSERTION:** every branch now receives a strict SUBSET of what it received before — `body` went from `In ∪ NotIn ∪ Is ∪ IsNot` to `NotIn ∪ Is`, `orelse` to `In ∪ IsNot` — so nothing that failed before can pass now. **MEASURED, eight guard spellings in a throwaway copy of the tree, `__pycache__` purged between every step, plus the real adapter as the control. Before → after:** correct membership `if key not in store: store[k]=v` PASS→PASS; correct sentinel `found=store.get(k); if found is None:` PASS→PASS; correct else-branch `if found is not None: revise() else: store[k]=v` PASS→PASS; unguarded store **FAIL→FAIL**, still naming its site (this is ARC 021's plant P4, re-run intact); guarded-but-emits-no-event **FAIL→FAIL** (arm 3 untouched); `if key in store: store[k]=v` **PASS→FAIL**; `if found is not None: store[k]=v` **PASS→FAIL** — that is this row's subject verbatim; and `if key not in store or self._force: store[k]=v` **PASS→FAIL**. **The last is a residual this row did not name and that building the table found rather than reasoning produced it:** a disjunction weakens the proof to nothing, and the old walk-every-`Compare` reader counted it as a guard because it never looked at how the tests were combined. **DOCTRINE B.4's OTHER EDGE, and this gate has already fallen off it once** — it reddened the correct implementation of its own subject on the first adapter it bound to. So the real adapter was re-run as a CONTROL after the change and its output is **byte-identical** to the output before it, exit 0 either way; arms 1 and 4 are untouched, the blast radius being one function and one branch read only by arms 2 and 3. **WHAT THIS EXPLICITLY DOES NOT DO: it does not bind the gate.** A stricter arm is still an arm that has never failed the real adapter. D3.9 and D3.10 are unaffected and `check_datafeed_bar_seal` remains UNBOUND in the ARC 022 census. **SWEEP RESULT ARC 023 (B2) — THE CONTROL WAS MIS-AIMED AS WELL AS ABSENT, AND THAT IS THE NEW PART.** D3.15 established that arm 4 measures nothing. The sweep asked the prior question — *would a working arm 4 have covered this residual?* — and the answer is **no**. This row's subject is guard POLARITY in the SOURCE (`if found is not None: store[k]=v`); arm 4 constructs published TYPES and drives immutability on them, a path an inverted guard in `_ingest_history` never reaches. **The row is NOT reopened**, and the argument is in the sweep section above: the ARC 022 discharge rests on the `_absent_proofs` strict-subset repair and its eight-spelling table, not on arm 4, and reopening it would make this ledger assert an unrepaired residual the measurement says is repaired. **RE-VERIFIED ARC 023 (S2) AND THE VERIFICATION UPGRADED THE EVIDENCE.** Two things were checked rather than assumed. (1) **The aim finding survives the existence finding.** Arm 4 now measures (D3.15 discharged), so the sweep's second claim was re-asked with a working arm — and it holds: arm 4 drives published TYPES and an inverted guard in `_ingest_history` is still on no path it executes. A control becoming real does not make it competent, which is the sweep's lesson surviving its own repair. The row is therefore still NOT reopened, on the argument already recorded above. (2) **The polarity detection was re-taken against the REAL adapter, which ARC 022 never did.** That arc's eight-spelling table was over synthetic spellings with the real adapter as a PASS control; here the plant lands in `broker_datafeed_ibkr.py:_ingest_history` itself — `if sealed is None:` inverted to `if sealed is not None:` — and the gate returns exit 1 naming `scripts/broker/broker_datafeed_ibkr.py:_ingest_history:1704`, control exit 0 either side, restore byte-identical by sha256. Under the rule of record at the head of this file that moves this property from demonstrated-against-a-fake to demonstrated-against-the-subject | discharged ARC 022 | verify |

The gate resolves its home from its own `__file__` — which is correct and is the non-restating spelling every other derivation in it uses — but its `__main__` block reads `sys.argv` only for `--probe`, so any other argument is discarded without a word. Every sibling gate in this registry (`check_order_path_bans`, `check_spec_citations`, `check_datafeed_granted_mode`, `check_datafeed_bar_seal`) takes a home path as an argument, so the convention invites exactly the mistake. **MEASURED, and it is why this row exists rather than a note:** the ARC 022 census planted the banked series figure 53 -> 47 in a throwaway copy of the tree and ran `check_derived_claims.py <scratch>` from this worktree. It printed `check_debt_open_items=53 [derived:ledger_rows=53, stated:series_table_latest_row=53]` and **exit 0** — a serene green over a planted defect, because it had measured a different tree entirely. The plant only fired when re-taken through the scratch tree's own copy of the gate. That is `debug.md` failure mode #12 — a proof taken in one environment presented as a claim about another — with the twist that the instrument names no environment at all, so nothing in the output distinguishes the two runs. Discharge: either accept a home argument like every sibling, or **refuse an unrecognised argument loudly** — the second is stricter, is three lines, and is the one doctrine C.7 asks for. Not done here because this gate is the tree's most heavily-consumed instrument and both sibling agents were live in adjacent worktrees when it was found | unassigned | verify |

### Promoted to doctrine — ARC 016

The recurring failure class **"a green light that measured nothing"** was promoted out of this ledger
into `debug.md` **§7.12** (doctrine v1.2.0), as a standing question required of every new gate —
***what would have to be true for this to pass while measuring nothing?*** — answered **in writing, at
the point the gate is built**. Failure mode **#14** (*scope set by an external mutable list*) was added
to the §8 catalogue. The seven-instance evidence base is tabulated in §7.12 and is not restated here.

> **Citation correction.** The ARC 016 brief directed this promotion at debt item **D2.8**. D2.8 is
> doctrine **B.7** — *no harness parses a constant out of a document and asserts the code equals it* —
> which is the **derive-never-restate** class, not the vacuous-pass class, and it remains **open and
> unassigned**. Nothing about it was discharged by the promotion. The items in this ledger that
> actually carry the promoted class are **D1.10** (each hook actually capable of failing), **D2.7**
> (nothing baselined on a pristine tree — bandit scanning nothing is its measured instance), **D2.12**
> (a suite that silently skips a gate reports GREEN) and the whole of **D3**, whose header states the
> principle outright. Those are the items §7.12 now stands over.
>
> Recorded rather than silently redirected, because a pointer that reads as authoritative while
> naming the wrong target is a **stale literal anchor** — `debug.md` §7.4, and the same class the
> ledger's own miscounted series row (instance #2 in §7.12) belongs to. Left uncorrected it would
> have produced a future arc "discharging D2.8" by writing a doctrine section that has nothing to do
> with parsing constants out of documents.

## D3 — Instruments whose can-fail has never been demonstrated

Doctrine C.2: a gate is guilty until shown able to say no.

| D3.7 | **the PINNED hook environment itself** — every `rev:` in `.pre-commit-config.yaml` | **opened ARC 019, open.** Split out of D1.10 rather than folded into `check_hook_suite`'s discharge, because it is a different property and folding it in would let that gate's green be read as covering it. D1.10 asked whether "the pinned `rev:` moves to an environment that scans nothing" is checked. `check_hook_suite` arm (4) answers half of it: the environment each hook resolves to *is* the store row keyed to the pinned rev, and every other resident environment for the same repo is named on every run — measured ARC 019, the store holds bandit at both `1.8.6` and `1.9.4` and the hooks resolve to `1.9.4`. The half it cannot answer is whether the **pinned** environment is non-vacuous. Nothing structural distinguishes a bandit that scans 21 files from one that raises on all 21 and exits 0; that is the ARC 006 defect verbatim, and the only thing that ever detected it was running the tool against a known-bad input. So this row is the D3 question asked of the *environment* rather than of the hook entry: for each pinned rev, drive a canary the hook must reject and observe it rejected. D3.1–D3.6 each did that once, by hand, at one rev; what is owed is the same demonstration re-taken whenever a pin moves, which is a bump-time obligation and not a boot-time one. Until then a pin remains a declaration, and `check_hook_suite`'s docstring says so where the gate is defined. **ASSESSED ARC 020 (C3) — REFUSED, WITH THE COST MEASURED RATHER THAN ESTIMATED, AND THE ROW STAYS OPEN.** The brief asked whether a per-hook canary is workable or worse than the debt. It was built and run, against a **throwaway repository** in a scratch directory — never `~/nix/.git/hooks`, which is shared by every linked worktree and would have disarmed a sibling agent mid-arc — carrying a copy of the config, a copy of `pyproject.toml`, and two planted fixtures. **The mechanism works and the runtime cost is not the objection:** `pre-commit run --all-files` over the pinned suite took **1.55 s wall**, against `check_hook_suite`'s ~1.5 s, so the cheap-enough argument is settled in the canary's favour and is not the reason this is refused. What the run measured instead is four costs, three of them structural: **(1) two generic fixtures caught only five of seven pinned hooks** — `ruff-check`, `pylint`, `mypy`, `bandit (production)` and `bandit (tests)` all Failed and named their site, while `ruff-format` and `complexipy` **Passed**, because neither fixture carried a defect they detect. A real canary therefore needs a fixture **per hook**, hand-authored against what each tool is believed to catch, which is a hand-maintained rubric of tool behaviour and goes stale silently the moment a pin moves a rule between preview and stable. That is the stale-anchor class this apparatus exists to remove, rebuilt inside the instrument built to remove it, and it is the same objection that made ARC 020 refuse a per-element confidence dimension one gate over. **(2) The canary repairs itself out of existence.** `ruff-check` runs with `--fix`, and the planted file's sha256 went `f7d415d0…f880f9` before the run to `ab4b045c…6e8de9` after it — the suite silently edited the evidence. So no static tracked fixture can serve; it must be re-materialised on every run, which is workable in a throwaway and impossible in the tree. **(3) The in-tree variant is blocked outright.** A fixture living in the real tree makes every `pre-commit run --all-files` permanently RED unless it is hidden by an `exclude:` in `.pre-commit-config.yaml` — and an `exclude:` that hides the canary from the real run is a scope narrowing indistinguishable from failure mode #14, which is the class the canary was supposed to close. That file is also outside sub-agent C's write scope, so the variant could not have been landed here in any case. **(4) The throwaway variant proves the pinned tool non-vacuous under a RECONSTRUCTED configuration, not the live one.** The copy must carry every config the hooks reference — `bandit` is invoked `-c pyproject.toml`, and `ruff`, `pylint` and `mypy` all read settings from that file. A copy that drifts measures a different tool configuration than the one that guards commits, which is failure mode #12 (a proof taken in one environment presented as a claim about another), and deriving the copy list rather than typing it reintroduces #14 inside the fix for #14. **One further finding, recorded so a future harness does not claim coverage it cannot have:** `pytest-affected` is `repo: local`, `language: system`, and carries **no `rev:` at all**, so it has no pinned environment to canary. In the throwaway it reported Failed for an environment reason (`./.venv/bin/python` not found), not because it caught a plant. A per-hook canary is structurally **seven of eight**, consistent with this row's subject being `rev:` pins, and any instrument built later must say so rather than let its green read as suite-wide. **WHAT WOULD MAKE THIS CLOSEABLE, and it is a different shape from a gate.** This row's own text already says the obligation is **bump-time, not boot-time**, and the measurement supports that: the property changes only when a `rev:` moves, so a canary run at boot and weekly buys nothing between bumps and actively converts a bump-time review into a background green nobody reads. What is missing is not the canary; it is the **trigger**. A bump-time trigger needs a banked fingerprint of the pinned revs and something that compares against it at an arc boundary — which is **D2.4** (nothing runs `verify.py` at an arc boundary and banks its verdict; Nix has no `bank.sh`), with **D2.5** (no known-red marker) beside it. **D3.7 is therefore downstream of D2.4, which was not previously recorded anywhere**, and that dependency is this assessment's main product. Closeable when three things hold together: D2.4 exists so a pin change is detectable as a change; one arc holds write scope over both `checks/**` and `.pre-commit-config.yaml`; and the fixture set can be derived from something the tools own rather than authored from what a person believes they catch — no tool in this suite ships such a corpus in a pinned, addressable form today | unassigned — bump-time, not boot-time: discharges with whichever arc next moves a `rev:` pin, and is blocked behind D2.4 for the trigger. A canary harness built without that trigger closes nothing | hooks |

| D3.15 | `check_datafeed_bar_seal` arm 4 — **the behavioural drive measures nothing** | **opened ARC 022 (C2), open, and it invalidates a justification two other rows lean on.** D2.20 and D2.21 each name arm 4's behavioural drive as the compensating control for a structural residual. Arm 4 reports `not synthesisable from annotations` for **every one of the five published types** — `Bar`, `BarRevision`, `FeedLag`, `FeedState`, `MarketDataMode` — so it constructs nothing, compares nothing and refuses nothing. **ROOT CAUSE MEASURED, one line:** `_synth_value` resolves an annotation by taking `ann.split("[")[0].split(".")[-1]`, which handles `float`, `int`, `str`, `bool` and enums but not a UNION. `Bar.volume` is `float | None`, and one unsynthesisable field makes the whole type unsynthesisable, so `Bar` is skipped for the sake of the single optional field on it. `FeedLag` is skipped for `declared_lag_s` and `observed_lag_s`, both `float | None`. **NOT A REGRESSION FROM THIS ARC — it was already true on the ARC 021 merged tree**, verified by running the ARC 021 gate against the ARC 021 tree and getting the identical four `not synthesisable` lines. So D1.14's claim that `FeedLag` *"is constructed twice from synthesised values differing in one field, and is proven to refuse a field write"* was true on sub-agent B's branch and stopped being true when the branches merged, and no instrument said so. The gate now re-executes arm 4 on every run and goes RED in **both** directions: red if the drive degrades further, and red the moment arm 4 is repaired and FeedLag is driven, because at that instant this row is payable and the registration must move with it. Discharging this row without flipping that entry is also RED, because `owed_row` is checked against the ledger's own bold-span rule. **DISCHARGED ARC 023 (S2), AND B3's REGISTRATION ANNOUNCED THE REPAIR BEFORE THIS CELL DID.** The union fix this row specifies is landed — `_union_members()` splits an annotation on its outermost `|` at bracket depth 0 and `_synth_value()` takes the first synthesisable non-`None` member, falling back to `None` only when no other member resolves. **THAT ALONE WAS A HALF-REPAIR AND THE MEASUREMENT SAID SO:** `Bar` began driving and `FeedLag` moved from `not synthesisable` to `not constructible — ValueError`, because `FeedLag.__post_init__` correctly refuses `observed_lag_s` set beside a provenance that is not `OBSERVED`, and the old synthesiser drew exactly one assignment (first candidate per field) and called the type unbuildable when that draw lost. Reporting `not constructible` about a correct type is doctrine B.4's forbidden direction one step short of an exit code, so `_synth` now SEARCHES: each field offers an ordered candidate list (every member of an enum; `None` last for an optional, so a field stays NUMERIC wherever it can), and the cartesian product is walked to `MAX_SYNTH_CANDIDATES=1024` until an assignment the type accepts is found — with exhaustion REPORTED as a note naming the count, never silent. **FOUR OUTPUTS, in a scratch copy of the tree, `__pycache__` purged between every step, and the plant chosen to ISOLATE arm 4:** control exit 0 with `arm4 scripts/broker/broker_seam.py:FeedLag: revision representable (differs in declared_lag_s), field write refused, value equality holds`; plant `@dataclass(frozen=True, eq=False)` on `FeedLag` -> exit 1 naming `scripts/broker/broker_seam.py:FeedLag: two identical values compare UNEQUAL — equality is identity, so 'unchanged' cannot be asserted either`; restore byte-identical by sha256; re-run exit 0. **`eq=False` RATHER THAN DROPPING `frozen=True`, DELIBERATELY:** the frozen plant also fails, but it fails through ARM 1, which reads the decorator statically — it would have proven arm 1 works and said nothing about arm 4. Equality is a property only arm 4 executes. **IT CANNOT BE A WEAKENING, and the argument is structural: arm 4 drove ZERO types before this change**, so no verdict can have moved from fail to pass; the arm went from five unmeasured types to two DRIVEN (`Bar`, `FeedLag`) and three honest notes — `BarRevision` because recursive synthesis of a composite field (`sealed: Bar`) is REFUSED rather than half-built, `FeedState`/`MarketDataMode` because an enum has no `AnnAssign` fields to synthesise at all. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, and this row's own second finding is why it is worth restating: it does not cover D2.21.** A repaired arm 4 still drives published TYPES and still cannot reach an inverted guard in `_ingest_history`. The script's own header asserted these "cannot be committed and cannot reach a diff". **REPAIRED**: both slashless spellings added, and the script now proves the ignore per target inside the worktree and exits 1 with the cause named, rather than asserting it in a comment. **Recorded rather than closed silently for two reasons.** First, this is the same defect as the `.testmondata` sidecars ten lines away in the same file — a bare pattern not covering every spelling of its subject, caught by ARC 016 there. The standing lesson is that a guarantee living in prose is not a guarantee, and the residual is that no gate yet asserts the ignore invariant outside the provisioning script's own run | unassigned — a gate asserting worktree ignore state | node |

It also means every future can-fail taken at the CLI is weaker evidence than it looks. **Discharge is small and is deliberately not taken here:** print `site` when it is non-empty, in all twelve blocks. It is twelve identical edits to the block `nix_check_contract.md` §4.2 forbids factoring into a helper, `check_datafeed_granted_mode` and `check_datafeed_bar_seal` are two of the twelve and are excluded from this arc's write scope by the B4 exclusion, and changing ten of twelve would leave the family inconsistent in exactly the way that makes the next reader trust the wrong one | unassigned — an arc holding all of `checks/**` with no datafeed gate in motion; strictness-only, no verdict changes | verify |

| D3.22 | `check_hook_suite` — **spawns `git` with no repository-selecting environment scrub** | **opened ARC 025 (C), open.** `git` honours `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` **ahead of `-C`**, and `pre-commit` exports `GIT_INDEX_FILE` — so a gate that shells to `git` from inside any git operation can enumerate a **different repository** than the one it was pointed at and report a confident verdict about the wrong tree. This is not hypothetical: it damaged sub-agent C's own worktree index during this arc before the cause was found, staging every tracked file as deleted. `check_artifact_gate_coverage` was repaired with a shared `git_env()` that strips nine such variables, and its test harness calls **the same function** so the hazard cannot be live on one side and invisible on the other. `check_hook_suite` was outside C's write scope and still has the exposure. **This is the project's recurring *ambient state sets a gate's scope* class arriving through the ENVIRONMENT rather than through a config file or a tracking state** — the fifth distinct shape. **DISCHARGED ARC 026 (B4), AND THE ROW NAMED THE WRONG SITE.** The sweep this row asked for was run over EVERY subprocess `git` invocation in `checks/` and `scripts/`, and it overturns this row's own premise: **`check_hook_suite` was never the live exposure.** Its `_clean_git_env` has stripped *every* `GIT_*` variable since ARC 019 — a strictly broader rule than the nine-name list `check_artifact_gate_coverage` was repaired with — so the gate this row is filed against was the best-protected of the three. **The live exposure was `scripts/runtime_gate.py`, which had no scrub at all and is the one program `pre-commit` invokes on every commit**, i.e. the single caller GUARANTEED to run with `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_INDEX_FILE` exported. It derives the Stage-3 gate's entire scope from `git ls-files` and its changed set from `git diff --name-only HEAD`, so an inherited index silently set what the commit gate measured. Reading the row instead of sweeping would have repaired the safe caller and left the dangerous one. **Repair:** one helper, `scripts/nixverify/gitenv.py`, applying the PREFIX rule (`GIT_*`) rather than a list — the three private spellings had already diverged by three variables, and a list does not inherit the selecting variables future git releases add. All five production callers and every fixture harness route through it. **Can-fail, committed, `scripts/tests/test_gitenv_hostile.py`:** two REAL repositories under `tmp_path`, every repository-selecting variable exported at the decoy, and a CONTROL arm proving the unscrubbed caller genuinely reports the decoy — without which the scrub assertions would be observing the right answer for a reason unrelated to the scrub. Disabling the shared scrub fails 7 of 10, each naming the REPOSITORY or PATH that answered, never an exit code; restored byte-identical by sha256 and all 10 re-pass. One of the seven is the fixture's own `git commit` landing in the wrong repository — ARC 025's actual casualty, reproduced under `tmp_path` | **discharged ARC 026 (B4)** | verify |
