| D3.150 | **NARROWED BY ARC 033 (0.2), NOT DISCHARGED — the mechanism exists and nothing calls it.** `scripts/nixrisk/positions.py`'s `PositionOriginWriter.on_fill` now takes `stop_distance` from the stop book's own `initial_distance_ticks` (the sizer's `stop_ticks`) and publishes the row on the SAME versioned snapshot, and `checks/check_origin_write.py` reddens when a published distance DISAGREES with the book's for the same trade — including on a value that is present, positive, plausible and WRONG, which a null-check would pass. **But `StopBook.arm` and `on_fill` both have ZERO production callers (D3.178), so production still never chooses one and this row stays OPEN.** Closing it on the strength of a built mechanism would be the exact move D3.136 was closed against — a decision recorded is not a mechanism landed, and a mechanism landed is not a mechanism CALLED. Original finding: **NOTHING IN PRODUCTION EVER CHOOSES A `stop_distance`. Every value on a published row in this tree is a test or gate literal, and the Phase-0.4 placeholder `20` is indistinguishable from a considered one** | ARC 032 (Stage 1 A), MEASURED by enumeration after the seam widened, not inferred | ARC 033. Exactly TWO production constructions of `PositionRow` exist: `nixrisk/picture.py:_decode_row`, which READS the field off the wire, and `nixrisk/flatten.py:_confirmed_rows`, which CARRIES it from the mirror's row and whose own comment says inventing one there would publish a §7:501 exposure no sizing pass ever computed. **No third site exists, so no code path assigns a distance at origin.** ARC 032 proved the field TRAVELS — seam, codec, real wire, mirror, and across a real process boundary — and proved nothing whatever about it being RIGHT; those are two different claims and only the first is banked. **The direction is still the permissive one:** §7:501 prices bucket exposure from the distance, so a row published with a placeholder feeds §7's cap a number no sizing pass computed, and a wrong-but-present value is not obviously safer than the absent one it replaced. Discharge = the origin write (the Limiter's fill path setting `stop_distance` from the accepted `Proposal`/`StopState`), PLUS a gate that reddens when a published row's distance disagrees with the stop book's `initial_distance_ticks` for the same trade. **Recorded now rather than after the fact, because the widening makes this the ONLY remaining hole between §7's cap and a real number** | ARC 033 | allocator |
| D3.151 | **The BUS and LATE-SUBSCRIBER arms of `check_picture_atomicity` JUDGE `stop_distance` but have NO per-run floor proving they could catch a tear on that axis** | ARC 032 (Stage 1 A), MEASURED by construction — `_arm_plants` enforces `MIN_STOP_TEARS` against the in-memory stop-book-join plant, and there is no equivalent floor for `_bus_race` or `_late_subscribers` | ARC 033. The in-memory races each carry a live plant and a floor; the wire does not, so its stop-axis power comes entirely from a can-fail control (`test_a_SINK_THAT_STRIPS_STOP_DISTANCE_reddens_the_WIRE_and_the_LATE_arms`) that runs in pytest and not in the gate. A run of the SHIPPED gate on a box where the suite is not run reports `0 torn` on the wire with no in-run evidence that a torn wire would have been seen — **so the two greens in one evidence string are not the same kind of green.** This is the asymmetry `MIN_STOP_TEARS` closed for the races, one transport out. Discharge = a bus-side plant with its own floor, or an explicit ruling that a gate may inherit its wire-arm power from its can-fail suite. Doctrine C.9 is the constraint: the answer must not be a second instrument for a property this gate already owns | ARC 033 | limiter |
| D3.152 | **A declared `RESOURCES` token that names nothing the check touches is UNFALSIFIABLE, and 24 of 50 checks carry at least one** | ARC 032 (Stage 1 B), MEASURED as a by-product of D3.140's both-interpreters sweep — every declared token run through the shipped `covers()` against the union of what that check was OBSERVED claiming under BOTH documented interpreters, 100 observations | ARC 033. `check_observed_resource_claims` owns exactly one direction — *"is what it said TRUE"* — which catches UNDER-declaration. The other direction is silent by construction: a token covering no observed claim is never a finding, so `RESOURCES` can name anything at all and this gate will not notice. Most of the 24 are honestly unfalsifiable — `interpreter:sys.modules`, `interpreter:sys.path`, `zmq-ipc`, `cpu-affinity`, `ptrace`, `shm`, `git-hooks`, `pre-commit-store`, `state/node_identity.json`, `network:pypi` name classes the PEP 578 observer does not watch, and a gate reddening on them would report its own blind spot as the subject's defect. **Two are INSIDE the vocabulary and were simply never exercised:** `check_runtime_gate` declares `subprocess:python` and was observed spawning only `subprocess:git` under both interpreters; `check_python_deps` declares `network:pypi` and made no non-loopback socket claim under either. Those two are indistinguishable, with this instrument, from a code path this box does not take — the residual the gate already records one level up. **NOT fixed here:** deleting a token because one run did not exercise it is the observer rewriting declarations to match what it just saw, which the gate's own `NON_CORRECTABLE_REASON` refuses by name. Discharge = decide whether an unexercised token is a finding at all, and if so which class may be judged, because the answer for `cpu-affinity` and the answer for `subprocess:python` are not the same answer | ARC 033 | verify |
| D3.153 | **`documented_interpreters` substitutes the OBSERVER's own venv when `nix_home` has none, so a wrong-tree venv would be swept under the label of the right one** | ARC 032 (Stage 1 B), MEASURED — the fallback fires on every synthetic population in the gate's own suite and is the reason its existing plant/control tests still measure anything | ARC 033. The venv launch mode is `<nix_home>/.venv/bin/python`. Against a synthetic tree there is no venv under `nix_home`, and returning a path that does not exist would make `check_observed_resource_claims` report "one launch mode unavailable" on every one of its own fixtures — **a gate blind on its fixtures measures nothing on them.** So when `nix_home` has no venv and the observer is itself running from one, THAT venv is used. **The residual, stated rather than implied:** `verify.py` pointed at a tree whose venv is genuinely missing, launched from some other tree's venv, sweeps the wrong venv under the label `venv`. It is not silent — the evidence line prints the requested path and what that interpreter reported itself to be — but it is not REFUSED either, and §17's rule is that a property proven while its subject is unavailable is not proven. Discharge = decide whether an absent `<nix_home>/.venv` is CANNOT_MEASURE (honest, and turns every synthetic-population test in that suite unmeasurable), or whether the fixture case is distinguishable from the production case by something better than *"a venv exists somewhere"* | ARC 033 | verify |

Recorded because the direction of the error is the safe one **only while nothing is parallel**: the first parallel block this tree emits will be evaluated against declarations that overstate what any one run claims, and the symptom will be a level refusing to go parallel for a reason no execution can reproduce. Discharge = decide whether `RESOURCES` should be able to say *"this token under this launch mode"*, or whether the plan should be derived per launch mode; both are structural and neither is a one-token repair | ARC 033 | verify |

ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'nixalloc.lifecycle'. Nothing was measured (§17)"*, including its non-vacuity test, while `python checks/check_allocator_caps.py` on the live tree stayed exit 0 — and MEASURED AGAIN at integration, when wiring the same module into `wiring.py` took 20 of `test_check_allocator_pathway.py`'s 22 controls the same way | ARC 033. **This is failure mode #14 one layer in: the suite's SCOPE is a list a person maintains, and the import graph is not.** The two instances above were repaired file by file (C consumed the screen structurally and imported under `TYPE_CHECKING` only, with the measurement recorded at the import site; the integrator added `lifecycle.py` to the pathway suite's `COPIED`), and neither repair is the fix. | D3.157 | **`verify.py --optimize --commit` cannot REGISTER a new check: the orphan rule errors before any plan is written, so every new check needs a hand edit to `checks/registry.json` first — the one thing the registry's own comment forbids** | ARC 032 (Stage 1 C), MEASURED — `--optimize --commit` with `checks/check_allocator_lifecycle.py` on disk and unregistered returns *"ERROR: check_allocator_lifecycle: present in checks/ but absent from registry.json — an ORPHAN check never runs and nothing says so"* / *"1 error(s) — no plan written"*, with the registry untouched | ARC 033. `checks/registry.json`'s own `comment` says *"Re-derive rather than editing this file by hand"*, and `nixverify/optimize.py`'s docstring makes the folder the scope precisely so an orphan is visible. **The two rules are individually right and jointly make the ADD case unreachable by the documented mechanism.** ARC 031's `599ff40` shows the same one-line hand seed for `check_allocator_caps`, so this is the standing practice and not a one-off. | D3.177 | **The `trade_id` <-> `client_order_id` relationship is UNDEFINED in the frozen spec, and ARC 033/0.2 shipped a DEFAULT BINDING in the absence of a ruling** | ARC 033 (0.2), MEASURED by enumeration: §3:159 keys the position table by `trade_id` and §4 mints it at open, while `StopState`, `ProposedOrder`, `Reservation` and §4's `(order_id, exec_id)` dedup tuple are ALL keyed by `client_order_id`. No code and no spec sentence joins them, so the arc brief's own success criterion — *"the published stop_distance for the same trade"* — was **not expressible** | unassigned — architect. This is the D3.137 shape (a seam that cannot express its own inputs) one module over. **The decision was made a SURFACE rather than an equality:** `seam.TradeOrigin` / `TradeOriginPort` declare it, `positions.EntryOrderOrigins` implements it, and the minting policy is an INJECTED callable whose default (`identity_trade_id` — a trade IS the entry order that opened it) is documented and overridable. That matters because under the default the two ids are EQUAL, so a writer that hard-coded the equality emits byte-identical rows and **no drive over the default can see it**; `check_origin_write` ARM JOIN re-drives the whole population under a NON-IDENTITY mint, which is the only way the defect is visible. Sound only inside the declared scope (intraday-only §6.1b, one strategy per instrument, one in-flight action per strategy §4); a scaling order, a position spanning a session, or a venue-side order-id re-issue each break the bijection. Discharge = the ruling, after which the default is ratified or replaced by one injection | ARC 034 | limiter |
| D3.178 | **Nothing in production ARMS a stop or CALLS the origin write — `StopBook.arm` and `PositionOriginWriter.on_fill` both have ZERO production callers** | ARC 033 (0.2), MEASURED by grep over `scripts/` minus `scripts/tests/`: `.arm(` appears in no non-test module, and the origin write's only callers are its own suite and its gate | ARC 034+. Discharge = the Limiter's `on_fill` handler arming the stop from the accepted `ProposedOrder` and then calling the origin write, in that order, on the same event | ARC 034 | limiter |
| D3.179 | **`PositionOriginWriter.unstopped()` has NO consumer: a confirmed fill with no armed stop is refused, recorded, and then nothing fires the §14 flatten it implies** | ARC 033 (0.2), MEASURED — the record is written and driven by the gate's UNSTOPPED arm; no production caller reads it | ARC 034+. §14 resolves every uncertainty toward FLAT and a filled position with no stop is an UNPROTECTED position (§4, §12.1). Recovered by re-reading each branch's file with `git show arc-036-<b>:docs/CHECK-DEBT.md` and re-appending every id absent from the merge; ledger 233 -> 248. **The class stays open even though this instance is closed**: nothing in this tree compares the merged ledger against the union of its parents, so the next multi-branch arc can lose rows the same way and stay green. A `git show`-based union check over the merge parents is the mechanism, and it is not built. **discharged ARC 037** (sub-agent F) by `checks/check_ledger_row_preservation.py`. The mechanism this row named is built: every commit reachable from `HEAD` is asked for its `docs/CHECK-DEBT.md` blob (`git rev-list HEAD` and NOT `git log -- <path>`, because log's history simplification prunes exactly the side branches a bad merge discards), the blob oids are de-duplicated, and the union of ids over all of them must be present in the working ledger. **THERE IS NO BASELINE FILE** — the comparison set is committed git objects, which the edit under judgement cannot reach — and a planted deletion plus a no-defect control are driven through the real comparison on EVERY run so a row grammar that stops matching is CANNOT_MEASURE rather than empty-agrees-with-empty. Every git failure is CANNOT_MEASURE, never PASS. Driven by plant: one deleted row, a deletion that is already COMMITTED, a `merge -s ours` that discards a branch's three rows, and fifteen at once — each names the D-number. **It reddened on its first run over the real history and the finding was real: D1.8 and D1.9 (D3.330)** | unassigned | verify |

Both halves are mandated: SEAM (b) fixes `raw(rank, n)` with `n = 8` producing ranks 1..8, and the brief fixes DENSE ranking so §6.6:455's equal-scores-are-FCFS holds between tied contenders. | D3.383 | **A plant/restore harness that restores with `git checkout --` silently DESTROYS the arc's own uncommitted repairs — measured, in this audit, on two frozen files** | ARC 038 (Stage 1 / sub-agent D), MEASURED the hard way | My first gate-plant harness planted a violation in `scripts/nixrisk/picture.py` and `scripts/nixrisk/survival.py`, ran the gate (both went RED and named their sites — the audit worked), then restored with `subprocess.run(["git", "checkout", "--", rel])`. That reverted each file to HEAD and **deleted this arc's four repairs**, which were uncommitted at the time; the harness's own sha-before/sha-after comparison caught it (`byte-identical=False`) only because I had printed both. A restore that reverts to the INDEX cannot distinguish *the plant* from *every change since the last commit*, and the failure is silent in the common case where the agent does not diff. This is the same class as D3.205/D3.344 — a tool reaching state the operator did not name — one layer over: not an inherited environment variable re-pointing a subprocess, but an inherited COMMIT re-pointing a restore. Repaired in my harness (in-memory `read_bytes()` snapshot, restore by `write_bytes`, SHA-256 asserted byte-identical) and the repairs were committed BEFORE re-driving, which is the second half of the lesson. **The class stays open:** nothing in the tree enumerates which plant harnesses restore from git and which from a snapshot, and `checks/` contains several that stage or mutate. Discharge = a shared plant/restore helper in `scripts/nixverify/` that snapshots bytes and asserts byte-identity, plus a sweep naming every harness that does not use it | ARC 039+ | verify |

| D3.432 | **The committed execution plan can silently disagree with the declarations it was derived from — nothing re-derives or compares at load, and it HAD drifted** | ARC 041-T (Stage 8), MEASURED: `verify.py --optimize` against `HEAD` = 41299aa proposed dropping `file-write:tmp` and `process:limiterd` from `level-0`'s `claims`, and `grep -rn 'file-write:tmp"\|process:limiterd' checks/*.py` returns NOTHING — no check has declared either since ARC 041 edited them, and the plan committed at `HEAD` still carried both | `scripts/nixverify/registry.py` LOADS the plan; it does not check the plan against the folder it was derived from — `grep -n 'stale\|derive\|declarations\|mismatch'` over that module is empty. `--optimize` is the only thing that compares, and it is run by hand, in whatever arc happens to add a check. So between derivations the `claims` list is a RESTATEMENT of the declarations (directive 3) with no mechanism keeping it true. The drift measured here is harmless — two surplus claim strings, which can only make the derivation MORE conservative — but the failure mode in the other direction is not: a check that ADDS a resource claim without a re-derive leaves a `parallel: true` block whose members' claims are no longer disjoint, which is exactly the property check-contract rule 5 says is 'checked, not taken'. It was checked once, at derivation time, and nothing rechecks it. Two other holes ride here and neither is closed: the freshness window (24 h) means an arc whose next sibling runs the following week is judged by a gate that has already declared its log stale, and `--live`, the only arm that would catch a watchdog still breathing RIGHT NOW, is deliberately unreachable from the sweep (a process scan at sweep time measures whatever else is on the box, not the arc the log describes). | D3.435 | **The producer census could not see §9's SOLE WRITER at all, and the repair excludes gate drivers by FILENAME SUFFIX — a spelling, not a shape** | ARC 042 (slice 4), MEASURED on the arc's own instrument, twice | (a) `check_plane1_event_coverage.producer_census` scanned `scripts/nixrisk/*.py` ONLY. §9 makes the **Limiter** the sole Plane-1 writer and the Limiter as a process is `scripts/limiterd.py`, one directory ABOVE the glob — so the census structurally could not observe a producer in the only module §9 authorises to be one. It did not fail silently: wiring the emitter made the gate red with *"RATCHET: §12.10 type(s) LOST their producer: go_timeout"*, a REGRESSION verdict over the arc that built the emitter — the ratchet reading its own blind spot as a loss. MEASURED, then closed by a `DRILL_SUFFIX` exclusion. **The residual is that the exclusion matches `_drill.py`, a NAMING CONVENTION.** A driver named `plane1_load.py` would be counted as a producer and no gate would notice — the same token-vs-mechanism class as D3.426 and D3.429, one layer out. Three candidate shapes were MEASURED on this tree and each one misclassified: (1) *constant-literal §9 fields* — the drills build rows with f-strings over a loop index, so all three read EMITTED beside `limiterd.py` and nothing is separated; (2) *creates its own Postgres substrate* (`initdb`/`pg_ctl`/`createdb`/`provision`, docstrings excluded) — separates the three Plane-1 drills cleanly from `limiterd.py`, `projection.py` and `drift_audit.py`, then MISSES `wal_kill_drill.py`, whose substrate is a WAL file and whose only emission is `EventKind.ACCEPTED`, so the repair re-creates exactly one free green — this row's own defect; (3) *spawned by a `checks/check_*.py`* — true of every drill AND of `scripts/limiterd.py`, which `check_go_timeout` spawns, so it separates the instruments from the one module §9 authorises to be a producer in the wrong direction. **A drill and a daemon are syntactically alike, and that is the finding.** `DRILL_SUFFIX` is therefore replaced by `GATE_DRIVERS`, a path→reason enumeration in the form this tree already uses for the same problem (`NOT_PRODUCERS`, `SQL_AUTHOR_EXEMPT`, `SINK_IMPL_EXEMPT`), plus `gate_driver_liveness`, which makes the census CANNOT_MEASURE if any enumerated path stops existing. That closes both halves of the suffix defect — it cannot CAPTURE a future `foo_drill.py` that is a real producer, and it cannot LOSE a renamed drill silently. Half (a), the census's blindness to `scripts/limiterd.py`, was already fixed in ARC 042. | ARC 04x | verify |

So a firing is booked on the NEXT tick, bounded by one tick interval, and `main` books once more after `run()` returns to catch the last tick of a CLEAN stop. `SIGKILL` has no such catch: the breaker can fire and the process vanish before the booking, and the money record will not know the GO was treated as DENIED. This is §9's own crash gap — *"crash gap healed by startup reconciliation vs broker truth"* — and that reconciliation is not built (`nixrisk/coldstart.py` is not wired into this daemon). | D3.438 | **Sole-writer enforcement stops at the OS user: any process running as `bbt` may still deliberately authenticate as `nix_limiter`** | ARC 043 (slice 5), BY CONSTRUCTION, stated at the moment the enforcement was built rather than discovered later | I8's enforcement has two layers and both are real: `plane1_hba.conf` makes the postmaster REJECT any connection to `nix_plane1` that does not name `nix_limiter` or `nix_reader` — a layer a SUPERUSER does not bypass, because pg_hba is evaluated before a role's privileges exist — and `plane1_enforcement.sql` keeps INSERT exclusive to the writer with UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE held by nobody. What it does NOT close is IMPERSONATION. The ident map must permit OS user `bbt` to become `nix_limiter`, because the sanctioned writer runs as that OS user, so a second process of the same OS user can pass `-U nix_limiter` and write. **A password was considered and REFUSED, with the reason recorded in the DDL:** a secret stored 0600 under the same OS user a rogue would run as is readable by the process it defends against, so it converts a one-flag bypass into a two-line one while costing a credential no fresh checkout has — calling that enforcement is the exact failure `plane1.sql` already refuses for triggers. The database cannot tell two processes of one OS user apart. ARC 043 closed the Postgres end. The WAL end is a file: `nixrisk/wal.py:290` opens it `open(self.path, "ab")`, so mode 0664 owner `bbt`, in `limiterd`'s runtime directory. A non-Limiter process running as the same OS user can append a record, and the sanctioned group-commit writer would then insert it as the Limiter — a forged row carrying the sole writer's own identity, which is worse than the S1 defect because it is not even a privilege violation. It is NOT open today, and the reason is not a defence: `Plane1PostgresSink` has no production constructor (its own docstring says so; only drills, gates and tests build one), so a forged WAL record has no live route into Postgres. That changes the day a daemon starts the writer. Same root cause as D3.438 — one OS user — and the same discharge closes both. | D3.440 | **`GATE_DRIVERS` is an enumeration, so a NEW instrument under `scripts/` counts as a producer until someone lists it** | ARC 043 (slice 5), the named residual of D3.435(b) | `check_plane1_event_coverage.producer_census` excludes gate drivers by an enumerated path→reason map because three candidate SHAPES were measured and each misclassified (see D3.435 for all three and what each got wrong). The reservation is HELD, counted on `held`, and named in `history()` with the reason — so it is visible, not silent. The residual is that NOTHING re-queries it: a genuinely never-placed order whose adapter has no record keeps its margin committed until the process restarts (§14's *restart = flat, always* is the only release), and Σ inflation from that source is invisible to §11.7 for the same reason every leak is. Measured direction: over-count, never under-count — it can deny entries, it can never breach a cap. With `--basetemp=/home/bbt/nix/scratchpad/pt_050` the destination lives inside the source, so each copy re-copies the growing destination: `scratchpad/pt_a/…/nix/scratchpad/pt_a/…/nix/scratchpad/…`, every level carrying `downloads/Pinokio-8.0.40-arm64.dmg` at 137 MB. Measured this arc: 620 GB in one basetemp and 108 GB in another, `/` at **100% full**, an `echo` failing with ENOSPC. Nothing was corrupted (`SESSION.md`, `RESULTS.md`, `CHECK-DEBT.md`, both JSON baselines and both new Python files were re-verified intact afterwards) but that was luck, not design — a write-back landing mid-fill would have truncated a banked file. **CLAUDE.md's ops pre-flight says "basetemp clean"; the sharper rule this measured is that basetemp must be OUTSIDE `~/nix` ENTIRELY**, because the tests copy `~/nix`. A cleaned basetemp inside the tree is still a recursive one. Discharge = either a `conftest.py` that REFUSES a basetemp under the canonical tree (cheap, mechanical, and check contract rule 8's "if a rule can be enforced mechanically, enforce it"), or an exclusion in the copy itself; the first is strictly better because it catches every future caller rather than one call site. | ARC 05x | verify |

The daemon line is the sharper half: an ABSENT signal instant is silently replaced with `time.time()`, so a GO that lost its stamp on the wire is indistinguishable from one issued this instant | Not called a defect here, and not called clean either. §6.4b scopes the monotonic guard to *"ALL venue-sourced state — balance, per-symbol margin, and position/quantity updates"*, and a GO is strategy-sourced: it arrives through no venue adapter, passes no `SourceMonotonicGuard`, and has no `decode_*` in this tree. §4:210-212 bounds the OTHER interval — admission to terminal feedback — on the loop's own monotonic tick clock (`nixrisk/loop.py::GoTimeout`), which is a different quantity from signal age and does not cover it. The frozen spec never says whether a signal's own age should bound entry, so inventing a threshold here would be a second authority for a §12A knob that does not exist. Admitted BY NAME in `checks/check_input_freshness.py::_ACCEPTED_UNGATED` as a one-way ratchet — a SECOND ungated time field appearing on a gate input is a FAIL, which is the property that ratchet exists to hold.
